The structural flakiness is unchanged and the item stays open on that basis; only the severity framing was wrong. It computes `latencies[(int) (iterations * 0.99)]` over a sorted 100-element array, i.e. `latencies[99]` — the **maximum**, not the p99 — so it asserts that the single slowest of 100 un-warmed `authorizePlayback` calls stays under 200 ms, a figure dominated by JIT warmup, GC and Testcontainers latency rather than by the code under test. Confirmed pre-existing during the deferred-16 code review by running it in a clean `git worktree` at HEAD `0d06925` (no review changes): fails at 267 ms. Observed 217 ms / 390 ms / 267 ms across three runs on one machine, and notably *worse* in isolation than inside the full suite. Fix is either a real percentile (`latencies[98]` for p99 of 100 samples, or more samples), a warmup loop before measuring, or dropping the wall-clock assertion in favour of a benchmark that is not part of the correctness gate. Left alone here because it is in the video module and entirely outside this story's scope. [`src/test/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/video/service/PlaybackServiceIT.java:106-120`]~~ **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-23 AC1]** Added a 20-call untimed warmup loop before the 100 measured iterations and corrected the index to `latencies[98]` (true nearest-rank p99 of 100 samples). Confirmed non-flaky: 3 standalone runs of the method plus a full-class run, all green. [`src/test/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/video/service/PlaybackServiceIT.java:105-124`]

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-deferred-17-booking-request-slot-payload-timezone-integrity (2026-08-06)

- **D1 — No session-duration cap: one click books the entire availability segment.** [CLOSED 2026-08-10 by `skillars-uat-2`] `AvailabilityService.computeAvailableSlots` (`AvailabilityService.java:214-245`) returns whole window-minus-block *segments*; there is no fixed-length slicing anywhere, and no duration bound exists in the booking package (grepped for `Duration.between` / `durationMinutes` / `sessionDuration` / `MAX_DURATION` — nothing). Backend validation is only: start in the future (`BookingService.java:171-174`), end after start (`:175-179`), range inside a window (`:182-186`). A coach with a 09:00–17:00 window and no blocks presents exactly one row, and one click books an 8-hour session that locks out the whole day via `findOverlappingBookings` and consumes one pack credit. Batch mode multiplies this by ten (`CreateBatchRequest.java:16`, `@Size(max = 10)`), and `BookingBatchService:106-113` performs no availability-window check at all. **Newly reachable, not newly introduced:** both submit paths were dead before deferred-17 (`requestedStartTime: undefined` → 400; `submitBatch` sent a timezone string into a `BigDecimal`). Real fix is slot slicing or a session-duration field — a product decision well outside this story. [Line citation refreshed 2026-08-07 by `skillars-deferred-18`, which edited `getAvailabilityCalendar`/`computeAvailableSlots` in the same file.] **CLOSED:** `AvailabilityService.computeAvailableSlots` now slices each free segment into consecutive fixed-length slots (`skillars-uat-2` AC2), and the length itself is a real concept — `marketplace.coach_pricing.session_duration_minutes` (nullable) over the `booking.session.defaultDurationMinutes` platform key, resolved by `SessionDurationResolver` (V93). Enforcement landed on **three** write paths, not the two this item named: `BookingService.createBookingRequest`, `BookingBatchService.createBatch` (which also gained the availability-window check it had never had) and `RescheduleService.requestReschedule`. The reschedule rule is deliberately different — same-as-*original* duration, not same-as-coach's-current-length — because durations were unconstrained until now, so a parent moving a legacy booking at its own length must not be hard-rejected; `BookingDuplicationService` stays exempt for the same reason. Grid alignment is deliberately NOT enforced (see AC3).
- **D3 — `formatSlot` hardcodes the `'en'` locale.** `BookingRequestPage.vue:280` — the pre-change `new Date(x).toLocaleString()` used the viewer's browser locale; `new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en', …)` pins English for everyone. The app demonstrably has German and French users (this same story added `de` and `fr-FR` translations). Left alone because AC4 explicitly prescribed mirroring `ParentBookingsPage.vue:184-190`, which hardcodes `'en'` too — fixing this page alone would create a new cross-page inconsistency. Systemic: also affects `SessionPackDashboardPage.vue:175-182`, `CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue:147-149`, `useTimezone.js:4-10`. Should be one sweep using vue-i18n's active `locale`. **[CLOSED 2026-08-12 by `skillars-uat-4` AC1]** This item's own file list was stale — re-verified against the tree at `6d1180c`, `SessionPackDashboardPage.vue:175-182` already calls `toLocaleString(undefined, …)` and was never actually broken. The real fix list (8 files, 10 call sites — `useTimezone.js`, `WeeklyCalendar.vue`, `CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue`, `CoachCommandCenterPage.vue`, `AvailabilityManagerPage.vue`, `ParentPlayerPortalPage.vue`, `ParentBookingsPage.vue`, `BookingRequestPage.vue`) now passes the active `locale.value` to every display-only `Intl.DateTimeFormat`/`toLocaleString` call. `useTimezone.js` has zero callers (dead code, left in place) and `CoachCommandCenterPage.vue:267`'s `getDayIndex` (matched against a hardcoded English weekday array) was deliberately left hardcoded — see the two new items below.
- ~~D4 — `AvailabilityManagerPage.vue:333` still sources its display timezone from `windows[0]`.~~ **[CLOSED — verified already fixed in code, found during skillars-deferred-23 story creation]** `AvailabilityManagerPage.vue:337` now reads `store.coachTimezone ?? 'UTC'`, not `windows[0].canonicalTimezone`, with a comment at `:333-336` explicitly citing this exact bug ("not from windows[0].canonicalTimezone... This also removes an unguarded windows[0] dereference"). No PR/story in this ledger's history claims this fix — it shipped unannotated. ORIGINAL: `coachTimezone.value = store.windows[0].canonicalTimezone ?? 'UTC'` reads `coach_availability_windows.canonical_timezone` — the exact independently-writable column deferred-17 AC4 exists to stop displaying from, one page over. The new `bookingStore.coachTimezone` (`booking.store.js:101,174,555`) is populated by the same `loadAvailability()` call this page already makes and is simply unused here. Same line also dereferences `windows[0]` unguarded (pre-existing). Outside AC4's scope, which named `BookingRequestPage.vue` only.
- **D6 — Zero frontend regression coverage for deferred-17's headline fix.** There is no frontend test suite in this repo (no `*.spec.js` outside `node_modules`, no `src/frontend/test`). Every test added or touched by deferred-17 is backend-only, so reverting the `.vue` and `booking.store.js` changes — the edits that actually made booking submission work at all — would leave the entire suite green. The story explicitly forbids introducing a test framework as part of its scope; this restates the standing gap already recorded for `skillars-5-4` W9.
- **D7 — `docker compose build` silently no-ops.** `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.local.yml` reference `image: ${APP_IMAGE}` (`skillars:local`) with no `build:` key in either file, so `docker compose build app` does nothing without erroring, leaving whatever image was last built manually. Surfaced by the deferred-17 dev's own live-verification session, where it cost significant time (the first pass falsely showed AC4 failing because the deployed jar predated the fix). Fix is either a `build:` block in `docker-compose.local.yml` or an explicit `docker build -t skillars:local .` step documented in `docs/deployment/local-deployment.md`. `[STALE — verified against current code by skillars-deferred-43 story creation, 2026-08-20: already fixed. docker-compose.local.yml:1-13's app service already carries a build: {context: ., dockerfile: Dockerfile} block, with an in-file comment explaining the exact hazard this item describes. Added by an earlier story, unannotated in this ledger.]`
- **D8 — Reconcile the two `canonical_timezone` columns to a single source of truth.** `coach_profiles.canonical_timezone` (`V26__marketplace_coach_profiles.sql:11`) and `coach_availability_windows.canonical_timezone` (`V26:55`) are independently writable — `CoachProfileService.java:90` sets the profile's from Step 1 and `:173` sets each window's from its own Step 4 payload, and `AvailabilityService.updateWindow` never re-syncs a window after a profile-level change. Deferred-17 AC4 worked around the divergence for one page by making display read the profile column; it did not remove the divergence. Deferred from the deferred-17 code review's D1 decision (Mbah, 2026-08-06: accept and document). Real fix needs a migration, a backfill rule for which value wins on existing rows, and a product decision on whether per-window zones are a deliberate feature (a coach who coaches across zones) or an accident of the profile-builder form. Note the display-side risk is largely contained once the slot label carries `timeZoneName` — instants are absolute, so no wrong moment is ever transmitted, only a differently-labelled one.

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-deferred-18-availability-slot-timezone-integrity (2026-08-07)

- ~~D1 — A DST gap can produce a zero-length or negative-duration slot in `computedSlots`.~~ **[CLOSED — verified already fixed in code, found during skillars-deferred-23 story creation]** `AvailabilityService.java:158-165` now carries exactly the guard this item asked for: `if (!windowEnd.isAfter(windowStart))` with a WARN log naming the coach/window/day, `continue`ing past the inverted window, plus a second WARN (`:176-183`) for the quieter "shortened, not inverted" DST case. The in-code comment restates this item's own Europe/Berlin 2026-03-29 example almost verbatim. No PR/story in this ledger's history claims the fix — it shipped unannotated. ORIGINAL: `AvailabilityService.java:117-118` materializes `windowStart`/`windowEnd` via `date.atTime(...).atZone(zone)`, and `LocalDateTime.atZone()` silently shifts a nonexistent local time forward by the gap length. The DB guard `chk_availability_time_order CHECK (end_time > start_time)` (`V26:56`) constrains only *local* times, so it cannot stop the instants inverting. Concrete: `Europe/Berlin`, window `02:30–03:00` on 2026-03-29 — `02:30` is in the spring-forward gap and resolves to `01:30Z`, while `03:00` resolves to `01:00Z`, so `windowStart > windowEnd`. `computeAvailableSlots` seeds `segments` with `{windowStart, windowEnd}` unconditionally and emits it, so the API returns an `AvailableSlotResponse` with `startDatetime` after `endDatetime`; the frontend renders it clickable and submitting hits `BookingService.java:178-182` ("end must be after start") → 400 behind a generic toast. **Pre-existing, not introduced:** for a single-window coach the outer zone and the window zone are the same value, so the identical inversion occurred before this story. Fix is a `windowEnd.isAfter(windowStart)` guard before the `computeAvailableSlots` call; deferred because the trigger requires a window whose local range straddles a DST gap (a ~02:30 Sunday coaching window), which no real coach is likely to have configured.
- **D2 — `blocks` week-scoping and the fetch bounds both hang off an unordered `findByCoachId`.** `AvailabilityService.java:85-86` derives `weekStartExact`/`weekEndExact` from `zoneId`, i.e. from `windows.get(0)`, and `CoachAvailabilityWindowRepository.findByCoachId` issues no `ORDER BY`. For a coach with windows in two zones, two identical requests can therefore return different `blocks` sets and use different fetch bounds purely from row-order variation; and a block that visibly punches a hole in a divergent-zone window's slot is absent from the response's `blocks` array, so `AvailabilityManagerPage.vue` renders a gap it cannot explain. This story narrowed the outer zone's role from a fetch bound to a response filter but did not remove the arbitrariness, which is the same divergence class as D8 below and is not independently fixable — picking a deterministic zone requires deciding *which* column is authoritative. Adding `ORDER BY id` to `findByCoachId` would make it deterministic-but-still-arbitrary, which is arguably worse (it hides the problem). Blocked on D8.
- **D3 — A parent's own pending request now makes the slot vanish rather than render disabled, with no affordance.** [CLOSED 2026-08-10 by `skillars-uat-2`] `BookingService.ACTIVE_SLOT_STATUSES` (reused by AC1's fetch) includes `REQUESTED`, `PAYMENT_PENDING` and `PAUSED`, whereas the frontend `bookedStartTimes` set this story deleted covered only `REQUESTED, ACCEPTED, CONFIRMED, UPCOMING, IN_PROGRESS`. So a slot held by the parent's own pending request — or by a `PAUSED` booking — is now simply absent from the list on their next visit, where before it rendered as a clickable-but-disabled row. The story's Dev Notes flag this as "unflagged-but-intended" and the backend behavior is correct (it matches the accept-path overlap check); what is missing is the UX replacement — a "you already requested this" state needs the booking data the page no longer loads, so restoring it is a product decision about the booking-request page, not a bug fix. **CLOSED:** `BookingRequestPage.vue` now loads `bookingStore.loadParentBookings()` on mount and merges the parent's own blocking bookings into the slot grid as disabled rows carrying a `BookingStateChip` and a `booking.requests.ownBookingCaption` caption. The backend is unchanged, as this item said it should be — `ACTIVE_SLOT_STATUSES` and the pseudo-block merge are untouched. Only tractable because of AC2: with fixed-length slots a booking occupies exactly one slot position, so the merged list reads as a continuous grid; against whole-segment slots the same merge produced overlapping rows of different lengths.
- **D4 — `@IanaTimezone` accepts fixed offsets, so its name and message overstate what it enforces.** `IanaTimezoneValidator.java:19` uses `ZoneId.of(value)`, which also accepts `"+05:00"`, `"Z"`, `"GMT+2"`, `"UTC+02:00"`. Those are not IANA region IDs and are DST-blind: a coach whose `canonical_timezone` is `"+01:00"` gets wall-clock times an hour wrong for half the year, and every consumer (`AvailabilityService`, `BookingService.isSlotWithinAvailabilityWindow`, the frontend `Intl` formatting) silently agrees on the wrong time. AC4 declared strictness out of scope and the 2026-08-07 review decision (Mbah) kept `ZoneId.of` and reworded the message/Javadoc instead of tightening, so the honesty gap is closed but the behaviour gap is not. Real fix is `ZoneId.of(v) instanceof java.time.ZoneRegion` (or `getAvailableZoneIds().contains(v)`), which needs its own test surface *and* an answer for values already stored — see the D10 audit-query decision in the story's Dev Notes, which deliberately ran no audit. Pairs with D5: tightening this makes D5 strictly worse.
- **D5 — the profile builder hard-400s on any zone the JVM's tzdb doesn't know.** `ProfileBuilderStep1.vue:90` and `ProfileBuilderStep4.vue:75` both send `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone` verbatim, with no zone picker and no fallback, and `@IanaTimezone` is now the only gate on that value. A browser on newer tzdata than the deployed JVM (`Europe/Kyiv`, added tzdata 2022b; `America/Ciudad_Juarez`, 2022g) makes Step 1 and Step 4 uncompletable, with a validation error about the coach's own machine and no way to override. Before this story the value was simply stored, so this is a newly reachable lockout, mitigated only by the deployed JDK 17 being patched recently enough. Fix options: an explicit zone picker in the profile builder, an alias/normalization fallback, or a documented floor on the runtime's tzdata version. Not urgent while the JDK is current; becomes a support incident the first time it isn't. `[STALE — verified against current code by skillars-deferred-45 story creation, 2026-08-20: already fixed. src/frontend/src/components/profileBuilder/TimezoneSelect.vue exists and is imported by both ProfileBuilderStep1.vue and ProfileBuilderStep4.vue — the server-validated zone picker this item's own "fix options" list named, shipped by skillars-uat-1 AC4 ("Fixed by a server-validated zone picker" per sprint-status.yaml). That closure was never tagged onto this original bullet. Added by an earlier story, unannotated in this ledger.]`
- **D6 — `ApiAdvice` can never resolve a non-English message bundle, so AC4's `messages_de`/`messages_fr` entries are inert.** `SecurityAdviceFilter.java:59` stores `locale.getDisplayLanguage()` — a *display name* like `"German"` — and `ApiAdvice.processFieldErrors:462` feeds it to `Locale.forLanguageTag(chosenLang)`. `Locale.forLanguageTag("German")` yields language `"german"`, not `"de"` (verified by execution 2026-08-07), so `messages_de.properties` is never selected; every field-error message resolves through the base `messages.properties` or the inline pipe fallback. This affects every custom validator in the codebase, not just `@IanaTimezone` — `validation.phone.*` has the same dead translations. It is also why the review could not close the "i18n keys are untested" gap with a test: there is no request shape that reaches the German bundle. Fix is to carry a real language tag (`locale.toLanguageTag()` or the `Locale` itself) instead of the display name; needs a sweep of all three `chosenLang` call sites in `ApiAdvice` plus `VideoApiAdvice:155`. **[CLOSED 2026-08-12 by `skillars-uat-4` AC2]** One-line fix at the single write site (`SecurityAdviceFilter.java:59`: `locale.getDisplayLanguage()` → `locale.toLanguageTag()`), plus the matching default in `RequestMetadata.java:39` (`"English"` → `"en-US"`). All 4 read sites (`ApiAdvice.java:434,461,498,620,626`, `VideoApiAdvice.java:155,158`) needed no change — they already called `Locale.forLanguageTag(chosenLang)` correctly and were only ever fed a broken input. Proven end-to-end, not just at the `ApiAdvice` unit: a new `CoachProfileBuilderIT` sibling test sends `Accept-Language: de` and asserts the response body contains the German sentence from `messages_de.properties:61`, not the English one — mutation-verified by reverting the `SecurityAdviceFilter` change alone and confirming the test fails.

## Deferred from: skillars-uat-1-admin-bootstrap-and-onboarding-unblock (2026-08-10)

Found while implementing the story. Each was examined and deliberately left alone; none blocks UAT.

- **D1 — ~~`MessagingService.getConversations` still has a silent-PLAYER fallback for an unrecognised role.~~** **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-22 AC1]** `getConversations` now checks `"PLAYER".equals(role)` as its own explicit branch, with a final `else` throwing `OperationNotAllowedException(MessagingErrorCode.NOT_A_PARTY)` — matching the class's three sibling exhaustive-dispatch sites exactly. `MessagingAccessControlIT.unrecognisedRole_yields403NotFatal` now asserts `getConversations` throws for an unrecognised role, alongside the pre-existing assertions for the other three sites. ORIGINAL: AC6 replaced the four `default ->` arms that threw `IllegalArgumentException`, but `getConversations` (`MessagingService.java:96-130`) selects its branch with an `if/else if/else` chain, not a `switch`: anything that is not `"COACH"` or `"PARENT"` falls into the `else`, which is the PLAYER path. So an unknown role there returns an **empty list**, not a 403 — the same silent-fallback class `skillars-deferred-16` set out to remove from `verifyIsParty`, surviving in a shape its `default`-arm sweep could not see. Found by an AC6 test asserting a throw that never came. Not fixed here: the `else` branch is the live code path for every real PLAYER caller, so converting it to a `switch` with a throwing default changes behaviour for real users and needs its own regression pass; and it is unreachable today because `MessagingResource.resolveRole` still guarantees one of three values. The durable fix for this whole family is a shared role enum instead of `String`, which would make all five sites structurally exhaustive.

- **D2 — two of AC6's four rewritten `default` arms are unreachable and therefore untested.** `resolveLastReadAt` (`MessagingService.java:439`) and `updateLastRead` (`:456`) both sit behind a `verifyIsParty` call that throws first — `updateLastRead` runs after the guard inside `getMessages`, and `resolveLastReadAt` is reached only from the summary mapper. No caller can drive either with an unrecognised role. Changing them was still correct (all four now raise the same type, so whichever a future caller reaches first behaves identically), but `MessagingAccessControlIT.unrecognisedRole_yields403NotFatal` deliberately asserts only the two reachable arms — `MessagingService.verifyIsParty` and `MessagingReportService.verifyIsParty` — rather than faking a reachability that does not exist. Recorded so a later reviewer does not read the gap as an oversight.

- **D3 — `AdminBootstrapRunner` cannot elevate an existing account, by design.** If the configured email already belongs to a coach, parent or player, the runner skips rather than granting `ROLE_ADMIN` to that row. This is deliberate (an "upgrade this user to admin" path is a different and riskier feature), but it means an operator who typos the address into an existing user's email gets a silent skip with only an INFO line to explain it. If admin provisioning ever becomes routine, it needs a real admin-management surface, not a wider bootstrap.

- ~~D4 — the admin surface is still API-only.~~ **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-23 AC2]** `TenantListPage.vue`, `TenantDetailPage.vue`, their two `routes.js` route entries, the `MainLayout.vue` "Tenants" nav item, and the entire `admin.api.js` Tenant Management block (14 functions) were deleted outright — the dead pages calling removed endpoints no longer exist. `HealthDashboardPage.vue` (the one admin page with a live backend) is untouched. The admin surface remaining API-only for anything beyond health is unchanged; building an actual admin UI is still a separate epic. ORIGINAL: `src/frontend/src/pages/admin/` contains a health dashboard plus `TenantListPage.vue`/`TenantDetailPage.vue`, and the tenant module was removed in `a170e69` — so those two pages and their routes (`routes.js:322-331`) are dead code calling endpoints that no longer exist. Not deleted here: unrelated cleanup that would have widened an already broad diff. `docs/deployment/uat-deployment.md` Step 8.3 documents the API-only reality so UAT testers do not report the admin account as broken. Building an actual admin UI is a separate epic.

They were left as-is: reformatting them would have produced a large diff unrelated to this story and buried the actual changes. The one new file, `TimezoneSelect.vue`, is Prettier-clean. A repo-wide `prettier --write` sweep is worth doing as its own commit — note `project-context.md` states Prettier is mandatory, so the codebase is currently in violation of its own rule. `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-27 AC6]` Ran `npx prettier --write` on exactly these 7 files (`AvailabilityManagerPage.vue`, `ProfileBuilderStep1.vue`, `ProfileBuilderStep4.vue`, `marketplace.api.js`, `i18n/{de-DE,en-US,fr-FR}/index.js`) — line-wrapping only, no translated content changed. ESLint and `prettier --check` both confirmed clean afterward. The wider ~124-file repo-wide Prettier debt remains untouched — a repo-wide sweep is still worth its own commit.

## Deferred from: skillars-uat-2-session-duration-and-booking-slot-integrity (2026-08-10)

Found or deliberately left while implementing the story. None blocks UAT.

- **D1 — `RescheduleService` performs no availability-window check at all.** Found while scoping AC3. `RescheduleService.requestReschedule` validates only future-start, end-after-start and (now) same-as-original duration; nothing checks that the proposed range falls inside a `coach_availability_windows` row, so a parent can move a session to 03:00 on a day the coach does not work. This is a distinct pre-existing defect from the duration one, and AC3 deliberately added duration enforcement here **without** the window check: adding it changes reschedule semantics — a coach who narrows their availability after accepting would retroactively block a legitimate reschedule of an already-agreed session — and it needs its own regression pass across `RescheduleServiceTest` and `RescheduleResourceIT`, neither of which seeds a window today. The same question governs `BookingDuplicationService`, which is exempt from AC3 for the identical reason. Decide the semantics first (does a reschedule have to fit *current* availability, or only *availability as it stood when the booking was made*?), then fix both together. `[PICKED UP by skillars-deferred-49 AC1, AC2]`

- **D2 — Booking error codes resolve nowhere, so every rejection is one generic toast.** `booking.slotUnavailable`, `booking.coachUnavailable`, `booking.duplicateSlotStartTime`, `booking.batchSizeExceeded` and now `booking.invalidSessionDuration` and `booking.overlappingSlots` appear in **none** of the four frontend bundles and **none** of the four `src/main/resources/i18n/messages*.properties` files — they travel to the client as raw codes, and `BookingRequestPage.submit()`'s catch shows `booking.requests.submitError` for every failure regardless. A parent rejected for a wrong session length sees "something went wrong" with no way to learn the coach runs 90-minute sessions. This story deliberately did **not** add a bundle entry for one code while its neighbours have none, nor build a per-code toast map — a half-fix is worse than either alternative. Worth its own small story once the error set stabilises. Note it interacts with `skillars-deferred-18` D6 (P1 #8): the backend bundles this would populate are exactly the ones `ApiAdvice` currently cannot select, so the backend half is inert until D6 is fixed. **[AUDIT 2026-08-17: the D6 interaction does not block the fix — `skillars-deferred-28` AC2 branches the frontend catch blocks on `errorMsg.errorKey` and renders the frontend's own vue-i18n string, never the backend-resolved `message` body, so the backend locale-selection bug is orthogonal. Also corrected: the sole existing precedent for this branching pattern, `DrillDetailPanel.vue`, branches on `helpCode` — a random per-request support ID, never an error code — so it has never actually fired; do not copy it verbatim. Scoped to the three parent-initiating flows only; the matching coach-side accept flows are a new, separate item below.]** `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-28 AC2]` Added all six `booking.*` keys (`coachUnavailable`, `slotUnavailable`, `invalidSessionDuration`, `batchSizeExceeded`, `duplicateSlotStartTime`, `overlappingSlots`) to the four backend `messages*.properties` bundles and a new `booking.errors` block to the three frontend `i18n/*/index.js` bundles. Wired the three parent-initiating catch blocks (`BookingRequestPage.vue`'s `submit()` and `submitBatchRequest()`, `ParentBookingsPage.vue`'s `submitReschedule()`) to branch on `err?.response?.data?.errorMsg?.errorKey` — each site limited to exactly the codes its throw site can actually produce (3 for `submit`, 4 for `submitBatchRequest`, 1 for `submitReschedule`; `createBatch` cannot throw `coachUnavailable`/`slotUnavailable`). Corrected a defect caught by story review before any code was written: the codebase's only existing precedent for this pattern, `DrillDetailPanel.vue`, branches on `helpCode` (a random per-request support ID) instead of `errorMsg.errorKey` and has therefore never fired — not copied. The three coach-side accept flows and `DrillDetailPanel.vue`'s identical bug remain open, logged as new items below.

- **D3 — `AvailabilityService.computeAvailableSlots` anchors its grid per segment, which makes slot start times shift when a coach edits a block.** Intended and documented (see the method's javadoc and AC2): the post-block run starts when the coach actually becomes free, so a 12:00–12:45 block yields 12:45, 13:45, … rather than discarding the 12:45–13:00 fragment. The consequence is that adding or removing a block changes the start times of every downstream slot that day, so a parent who had a specific hour in mind may not find it after a coach edits their calendar. AC3 explicitly declines to enforce grid alignment on the write paths for this reason — an alignment rule would retroactively invalidate a booking whose slot was legal when it was made, and `RescheduleService`/`BookingDuplicationService` both write times derived from existing bookings. If UAT feedback says the shifting grid is confusing, the fix is a product decision (window-anchored grid, accepting the dropped fragments), not a bug fix.

- **D4 — the batch path still has no create-time cross-booking overlap check.** The single path has one (`BookingService.createBookingRequest`); `createBatch` does not, and AC4 deliberately left it that way. Batch rows are created `REQUESTED`, and `V87`'s exclusion constraint deliberately excludes `REQUESTED` — its own comment records that two overlapping `REQUESTED` bookings competing for a slot is expected in-band behaviour that the accept-time re-check resolves, and `acceptAll` already runs that re-check against `ACTIVE_SLOT_STATUSES_EXCLUDING_REQUESTED`. Adding a create-time check would reject legitimate competing requests. Recorded so a later reviewer does not read the asymmetry as an oversight. AC4 *did* add the intra-batch overlap check (two slots in the same batch overlapping each other), which is a different and unambiguous case.

- **D5 — `ConfigService`'s 5-minute cache TTL makes a platform-default change look like it did nothing.** `booking.session.defaultDurationMinutes` is read through `ConfigService.getBoundedLong`, and `ConfigService` refreshes on a `@Scheduled(fixedDelayString = "${app.config.cache-ttl-seconds:300}")`. An admin who changes the value and immediately reloads the availability calendar sees the old slot length and will reasonably conclude the setting is broken. `updateConfig` does call `invalidate()`, so a change made *through the config API* is immediate; a change made directly in the database is not. Documented in the V93 migration comment; a real fix is either an admin-UI note or narrowing the TTL, both of which are wider than this story.

- **D6 — no frontend test coverage for any of this story's `.vue` changes.** Standing gap (`skillars-deferred-17` D6, `skillars-5-4` W9, `skillars-uat-1`): there is no frontend test suite in this repo, so `ProfileBuilderStep3.vue`'s duration select, `BookingRequestPage.vue`'s merged own-booking rows and coach-timezone week bounds, and `ParentBookingsPage.vue`'s derived read-only reschedule end are verified by code reading and a successful production build only. Reverting all three would leave the entire suite green.

- **D7 — `docker-compose.local.yml` now needs a `redis` override it did not need before.** AC6 replaced the `redis-data` named volume with a bind mount at `/opt/skillars/data/redis`, which is a production path. `docker-compose.local.yml` overrides the volume of every other stateful service but had never needed one for redis, so without the new `skillars-local-redis` override added here, running the local stack would have created `/opt/skillars/data/redis` on a developer's own machine. Fixed in this story; recorded because it is the general hazard of putting absolute host paths in the base compose file — any future service that moves onto the Volume needs the same treatment, and nothing enforces it.

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-uat-2-session-duration-and-booking-slot-integrity — Group A (2026-08-10)

Backend duration-model files only (`SessionDurationResolver`, `V93` migration, `AvailabilityService`, `BookingService`, `BookingBatchService`, `RescheduleService`, `BookingError`, `ProfileBuilderStep3Request`, `CoachPricing`, `CoachProfileService`), reviewed against AC1–AC4. Group B (backend tests) also reviewed, no additional defers there. Group D (ops/infra/docs) pending.

- **Session duration / availability windows are resolved once and not re-validated before persistence.** `BookingBatchService.createBatch` resolves `requiredDuration` and fetches `windows` once before the per-slot loop and reuses both for the whole batch; the actual writes happen afterward via `TransactionTemplate`. Separately, `AvailabilityService.getAvailabilityCalendar`'s resolved `slotLength` and the later `BookingService`/`BookingBatchService` calls that independently re-resolve `requiredDuration` have no version or ETag tying a GET availability response to a subsequent POST booking — a coach changing their session length in between can turn a slot the parent just saw into a rejection. Both are the same underlying class of read-then-write staleness that already exists elsewhere in the booking module (e.g. availability windows/blocks changing between GET and POST); not newly introduced by this story and not actionable within its scope.
- **`V93__session_duration.sql` validates its new `CHECK` constraint in the same `ALTER TABLE` as the `ADD COLUMN`,** taking an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on `marketplace.coach_pricing` for the scan rather than splitting into `ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK (...) NOT VALID` followed by a separate `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT`. Low practical impact at `coach_pricing`'s expected row count (one row per coach) and consistent with this repo's other migrations, but worth a shared migration-pattern policy if a future migration targets a larger table.

Frontend + i18n files only (`ProfileBuilderStep3.vue`, `BookingRequestPage.vue`, `ParentBookingsPage.vue`, four i18n bundles), reviewed against AC1/AC3/AC5.

Not fixable within a frontend-only diff; would need the status set exposed via an API contract or generated from a shared source. `[PICKED UP by skillars-deferred-47 AC1, AC2]`
- **The session-duration `q-select` (`ProfileBuilderStep3.vue`) offers only 5 discrete values (30/45/60/90/120 minutes, plus "platform default") while the backend/DB accept any integer 15–240** (`chk_coach_pricing_session_duration`, V93). A coach whose `session_duration_minutes` was set outside that set — via a direct API call rather than this dropdown — would see the select render unselected. Narrow and low-probability; needs a product decision on how to surface an out-of-list value (show the raw number? add a synthetic option?) rather than a unilateral fix.

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-uat-2-session-duration-and-booking-slot-integrity — Group D (2026-08-11)

Ops/infra/docs files only (`ci.yml`, `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.local.yml`, `provision.sh`, `deploy/traefik/README.md`, `docs/deployment/*.md`, `sprint-status.yaml`, `uat-readiness-priorities.md`), reviewed against AC6/AC7. `deferred-work.md` itself excluded (reviewed separately, see Groups A–C above).

- ~~`runbook.md`'s "clear Redis data" emergency procedure targets `appendonly.aof`/`dump.rdb`, which don't match Redis 7's default Multi-Part AOF layout~~ **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-23 AC4]** `runbook.md:225` now runs `rm -rf /opt/skillars/data/redis/appendonlydir /opt/skillars/data/redis/dump.rdb`, matching Redis 7's default Multi-Part AOF layout (no `--appenddirname` override in `docker-compose.yml`). ORIGINAL: (`appendonlydir/` containing `*.base.rdb`/`*.incr.aof`/`*.manifest` files instead). Pre-existing — the same wrong filenames were already referenced before this story, just accessed via a `docker run -v ... alpine rm -f` indirection rather than a direct path — so not introduced by `skillars-uat-2`, but worth knowing before anyone actually runs the procedure: as written it would silently no-op on Redis 7's real AOF data.
- **If `provision.sh` is run once before the Hetzner Volume is attached, then the volume is attached and the script rerun later, the pre-volume `acme.json` (created on the root disk by section 7.5's unconditional path) is orphaned rather than migrated onto the newly-mounted volume.** The story's own migration note in `uat-deployment.md` covers the pre-story path (`/opt/skillars/traefik/acme.json` → `/opt/skillars/data/traefik/acme.json`), not this specific "provisioned without volume, then volume attached later" sequence. Narrow, low-probability ops scenario — only bites an operator who provisions before attaching the Volume.
- ~~`.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s new `:latest` tag has no explicit `concurrency` group.~~ **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-23 AC5]** `build-and-push` (`ci.yml:49-53`) now carries `concurrency: {group: build-and-push-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: false}`. **Residual gap, not fully closed:** `build-and-push` still declares `needs: test`, so concurrency-slot readiness tracks `test`-job duration, not push order — a slower older push's job can still complete its `build-and-push` after a faster newer push's, still overwriting `:latest` with the older commit. The `concurrency` block prevents two `build-and-push` runs from interleaving mid-upload (a real improvement); it does not guarantee the newest-pushed commit wins `:latest`. A stronger fix (`build-and-push` checking `git merge-base --is-ancestor` against the currently-published `:latest` digest before overwriting) is out of scope; SHA tags are unaffected and `rollback.md` pins to those, not `:latest`. ORIGINAL: Two `master` pushes whose `build-and-push` jobs complete out of trigger order could theoretically leave `:latest` pointing at an older commit than the newest push. Narrow race (push-to-`master` only, no PR builds trigger this job) and low practical impact, but worth a `concurrency:` block if `:latest` becomes load-bearing for anything beyond routine redeploy convenience.

## Deferred from: skillars-uat-3-payment-capture-integrity-and-backup-retention (2026-08-11)

Found or deliberately left while implementing the story. The first item is an **operational finding, not a code defect, and it is the most consequential thing in this list.**

- **D1 — ~~`volume-snapshot.sh` calls an endpoint that does not exist, so the Hetzner Volume at `/opt/skillars/data` has NO working backup.~~** **[CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `skillars-uat-6` AC5-AC7]** `volume-snapshot.sh` and `restore-from-snapshot.sh` are deleted. `deploy/backup/volume-backup.sh` now archives everything under `/opt/skillars/data` except `postgres/` (already covered by `pg-backup.sh`) to Hetzner Object Storage daily at 02:00 UTC — verified the `tar --exclude='./postgres'` pattern actually excludes it via `tar tzf` inspection before treating this as done. `deploy/backup/restore-from-volume-backup.sh` restores it, including the redis/traefik ownership steps the old restore script omitted. `prune-backups.sh`'s dead `prune_snapshots()` branch (see D10/D16 and the two pagination-gap notes below, all closed by the same removal) was replaced with `prune_volume_backups()` mirroring the working S3-dump pruner. ORIGINAL: `volume-snapshot.sh` calls an endpoint that does not exist, so the Hetzner Volume at `/opt/skillars/data` has NO working backup. Found while satisfying AC6's instruction to verify the Hetzner list/delete endpoints against live API docs before writing the pruner. **Verified 2026-08-11 against four independent sources:** the Hetzner Cloud Python SDK (generated from the API spec) exposes only `attach`/`detach`/`resize`/`change_protection`/`create`/`delete`/`update` on `VolumesClient` and no snapshot method; the Hetzner Cloud API changelog contains no mention of volume snapshots; Pulumi's `hcloud.Snapshot` takes a `ServerId` and does not support volumes; and Hetzner's own guidance states that server snapshots explicitly **exclude** attached volumes and that a native snapshot of an external volume cannot be created. `volume-snapshot.sh` POSTs to `/v1/volumes/${HETZNER_VOLUME_ID}/actions/create_snapshot`, which therefore 404s, and the script's own HTTP-code check exits 1 — every cron run since `deploy-3-1`. This is consistent with `drill-log.md` containing only its placeholder row: no restore drill has ever been run, which is exactly what would have caught it. **Consequence:** postgres data, redis AOF, prometheus, loki, grafana, tempo and `acme.json` all live on that volume and none of them is backed up. The Object Storage `pg_dump` stream (`pg-backup.sh`) is unaffected and is the only working backup — of the database only. `restore-from-snapshot.sh` and Section B of `backup-restore.md` rest on the same non-existent snapshots. **Deliberately not fixed here:** choosing the replacement is an operational decision (file-level backup of the volume to Object Storage, a Hetzner Storage Box, or a documented decision to accept dumps-only), and it is a deployment story, not a payment one. Loud warnings added to `volume-snapshot.sh`, `backup-restore.md` and `runbook.md`; `prune-backups.sh`'s snapshot half is written against the real images API (`GET /v1/images?type=snapshot`, `DELETE /v1/images/{id}`) so it is correct for any snapshot that does exist, and it emits a `[warn]` on a zero match rather than exiting quietly, because zero is precisely what a broken producer looks like. [`deploy/backup/volume-snapshot.sh`, `deploy/backup/restore-from-snapshot.sh`, `docs/deployment/backup-restore.md`]

- **D2 — ~~no Stripe idempotency key on `createPaymentIntent`.~~** **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-22 AC2]** `createPaymentIntent` now accepts an idempotency key, built by `StripePaymentGateway.chargeAndCapture` as `"pi-" + referenceId + "-" + parentId + "-" + <1-minute time bucket>`. The time bucket was added beyond the original ledger item's scope after code review surfaced a real collision: `referenceId` alone (or `referenceId`+`parentId` alone) is stable across two genuinely separate purchases of the same session-pack tier by the same parent, which would have made the second purchase silently replay the first `PaymentIntent` instead of charging again. The bucket keeps true retries (same attempt, same minute) deduped while letting a later, separate purchase get a fresh key. ORIGINAL: `StripeClient.createSubscription:64-70` already passes `RequestOptions.setIdempotencyKey(...)`; `createPaymentIntent:28-30` does not. A `PaymentGatewayException` raised by a network timeout may mean "charged" or "not charged", and the failure path writes `CHARGE_FAILED` either way. AC1's reservation narrows the window enormously — there is now always a durable row saying a charge was attempted — but this is the last remaining ambiguity inside the capture path, and a booking-id-derived key would close it outright by making a retry provably the same charge. Unchanged by this story. [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/payment/service/StripeClient.java:28-30,64-70`]

- **D3 — `CAPTURE_PENDING` has no automated exit.** A reserved row that never completes blocks the parent's cancel (AC2 returns 409 for as long as it stands), holds the coach's slot, and is refused by the sweeper. AC5 escalates it on every 15-minute sweep via `booking.payment_pending.unrecoverable{reason="CAPTURE_UNCONFIRMED"}` and Scenario 4 of `runbook.md` documents the manual resolution, but nothing times it out. **Deliberate.** Every automatic option is worse: declining could charge a parent for nothing, confirming could give away an unpaid session, and re-charging could take the money twice. Only a human can read the Stripe side. Recorded so the absence reads as a decision rather than an oversight.

- **D4 — `POST /api/bookings/{id}/cancel` has no frontend caller: a parent cannot cancel through the app at all.** `booking.api.js:64` exports `cancelBooking` and grepping `cancelBooking` across `src/frontend/src` returns that one line and nothing else — no page, no store, no component invokes it (`ParentBookingsPage.vue`'s only `cancel` hit is a `q-btn` closing a dialog). This is a parent-journey gap with its own design (confirmation step, refund preview showing whether the >24h rule applies, coach notification), not a wiring fix, which is why AC2 hardened the endpoint without building the UI. It also means the race AC2 closes was reachable by direct API call only — recorded on `deferred-12` D2 above as a correction to `uat-readiness-priorities.md`'s framing.

- **D5 — `DisputeService`'s payment lookups are unguarded on status.** `DisputeService:133,168` read `booking_payments` with a bare `findById` and would now happily read a `CAPTURE_PENDING` row. Safe **only** because `DISPUTED` is unreachable from `PAYMENT_PENDING`: `BookingStateMachine:54-75` allows `DISPUTE` only from `IN_PROGRESS`/`PAUSED`/`COMPLETED*`, all downstream of `CONFIRMED`, by which point any reservation has been written through to a terminal status. Recorded as a tripwire — a future state-machine edit that makes `DISPUTE` reachable earlier would silently turn a pre-capture row into a dispute amount. The receipt paths (`RevenueReportingService.getCoachReceipt`/`getParentReceipt`) were the reachable instance of this class and **were** guarded by AC1. `[PICKED UP by skillars-deferred-41 AC1]`

- ~~D6 — `SessionPackPurchaseRepository.findByIdForUpdate:17-20` has no `jakarta.persistence.lock.timeout` hint~~, unlike the three sibling `findByIdForUpdate` methods it otherwise mirrors (`CoachProfileRepository:28-34`, `BookingRescheduleRequestRepository:26-31`, and now `BookingRepository`). **[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-23 AC6 — annotation added, underlying claim found false]** The identical `@QueryHints(@QueryHint(name = "jakarta.persistence.lock.timeout", value = "5000"))` was added and the redundant `@Transactional` removed, matching the three siblings' shape exactly. **However, implementing this AC's own test requirement (prove the timeout bounds the wait) disproved the premise this item and its three siblings share**: Hibernate 6.6.53's `PostgreSQLDialect.withTimeout()` only special-cases the `NO_WAIT`/`SKIP_LOCKED` sentinels — any finite millisecond value, including `"5000"` on all four repositories, falls through unchanged and has zero effect on Postgres. Confirmed empirically in a real IT: a contended `findByIdForUpdate` call blocked for the full duration (tested to 12s) a competing lock was held and then completed normally, with no `PessimisticLockingFailureException` ever raised — `ApiAdvice.pessimisticLockExceptionHandler`'s 409 mapping is not reachable via this path on any of the four repositories. **New follow-up item filed below** (this same "Deferred from" run) since fixing it is out of AC6's scope. What AC6's new test (`SessionPackPurchaseLockContentionIT`) actually proves instead: the `@Lock(PESSIMISTIC_WRITE)` mutex itself still correctly serializes concurrent access (no lost update), mutation-verified by removing the annotation and confirming the test fails. ORIGINAL: Contention on a pack row therefore blocks the request thread indefinitely instead of surfacing as `ApiAdvice.pessimisticLockExceptionHandler`'s clean 409. It also carries an extra `@Transactional` the others do not. Pre-existing, untouched here, and a three-line fix — but harmonising the four is its own change with its own regression pass, and this story deliberately did not "tidy" a lock idiom while adding a fourth user of it.

- **D7 — `PaymentWebhookIdempotencyIT` seeded no `booking.bookings` rows at all.** Found in Task 0's triage; the story's regression table predicted only that it might assert "exactly N rows". The class mocks `BookingService` specifically so transitions do not need a real booking, which meant `reserveCapture`'s locked read found nothing, returned `BOOKING_NOT_PENDING`, and left zero payment rows. Fixed by seeding a real `PAYMENT_PENDING` booking in the one test that reaches Stripe — the fixture, not the check, per `uat-2`'s recorded lesson. Recorded because it generalises: **any test that drives a settlement path now needs a real booking row**, which was not true before this story, and the other two tests in that class pass only because they never reach Stripe (full credit cover and pack-funded both skip the reservation).

- **D8 — the batch reservation records the whole price under `stripeCharged`, before the wallet split is known.** AC3 reserves per booking during the pricing loop, but a booking's credit/Stripe split is not computed until the wallet balance is read for the batch as a whole. The reserved row therefore carries `creditDebited = 0` and `stripeCharged = price`, and the settle write-back corrects both. This is sound because only `CAPTURED` rows are ever summed by the revenue queries (pinned by `CaptureReservationIT#capturePendingRowIsInvisibleToEveryRevenueQuery`), so a reserved row's amounts are an operator's reconciliation hint rather than an accounting record — but an operator reading a stuck batch row in the runbook's Scenario 4 should know the figures are an upper bound on the Stripe leg, not the actual split. Restructuring to reserve after the split would mean either two passes over the wallet or reserving after the charge, both worse. `[STALE — verified against current code by skillars-deferred-43 story creation, 2026-08-20: already fixed. docs/deployment/runbook.md:321-324 already carries a blockquote directly above Scenario 4's detection query stating the credit_debited/stripe_charged columns on a CAPTURE_PENDING row are a reconciliation hint, not an accounting record, covering exactly the single-vs-batch distinction and the upper-bound-not-actual-split caveat this item asked for. Added by an earlier story, unannotated in this ledger.]`

- **D9 — no frontend test coverage.** Standing gap (`uat-1`, `uat-2` D6, `deferred-17` D6, `deferred-18`): there is no frontend test suite in this repo. This story touches **no** `.vue` file, so unlike its two predecessors nothing here is verified by code reading alone — recorded only to note that the gap is unchanged, not that it bit this story.

- **D10 — ~~`prune-backups.sh` is unexercised against a live endpoint.~~** **[CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `skillars-uat-6` AC6 — mechanism removed]** The `jq` dependency this item flagged was required only by `prune_snapshots()`, which `skillars-uat-6` AC6 deletes in full (along with its `command -v jq` check and call site) — the dependency no longer exists in this script. Not conditional on anything else: `provision.sh`'s apt list already includes `jq` regardless (added before this story, confirmed still present at `provision.sh:22`), so the "already-provisioned Node" caveat this item raised was never blocking. The broader "unexercised against a live endpoint" posture is unchanged and remains open — see `skillars-uat-6`'s own STILL OPEN note for `volume-backup.sh`/`prune-backups.sh`'s first live run. ORIGINAL: `prune-backups.sh` is unexercised against a live endpoint. `shellcheck` passes on it and on all three siblings, and its logic was read carefully, but neither half has been run: the S3 half needs Hetzner Object Storage credentials and the snapshot half needs a live `HCLOUD_TOKEN`, neither of which exists in this environment. The first production run must be `--dry-run` (documented in `backup-restore.md`). It also newly requires `jq`, which was added to `provision.sh`'s apt list — **an already-provisioned Node will not have it**, and the snapshot half hard-fails with a clear message rather than silently skipping; run `apt-get install -y jq` on the existing Node before installing the cron.

If the two land in the same narrow window, the settle write can block on cancel's lock for however long the connection allows, rather than surfacing as `ApiAdvice`'s clean 409. Architectural — spans both the cancel and settle paths — not a regression introduced by this diff, which only added the interlock check itself. [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/payment/service/BookingPaymentPersistenceService.java:73-105`]

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-uat-3-payment-capture-integrity-and-backup-retention (2026-08-11)

All 13 patch findings were applied; these are the review's own deferrals plus one item the patch round produced.

- **D11 (from the patch round, not the review) — `PaymentPendingSweeper.sweepOne`'s row lock is justified by reasoning, not by a test.** The review correctly found that `sweepOne` decided on the *absence* of a payment row and then wrote one, while `reserveCapture` concurrently decided on the absence of the same row and inserted `CAPTURE_PENDING` — so an unlocked sweeper could commit `CHARGE_FAILED` over a granted reservation (`save()` on an assigned `@Id` with no `@Version` is a `merge()`, i.e. an UPDATE, not a failing INSERT). Fixed by re-reading under `findByIdForUpdate`, the same lock `reserveCapture` takes. **The IT written to prove it was deleted for passing unchanged against the unlocked code**: both threads start on one latch, but the sweeper first reads config and runs the stranded-booking query while `reserveCapture` goes almost straight to its insert, so the reservation committed first every time and the correct outcome came from the payment-row check rather than the lock. That the six existing `PaymentPendingSweeperTest` cases all broke when the lock landed does confirm the production path changed, but a mock swap is not proof of serialisation. The lock's read-then-write window is microseconds wide and not reachable from a test at this level; proving it would need either production instrumentation (a test-only hook inside `sweepOne`) or a DB-level fault injector, both of which are their own change. Recorded rather than left implicit, because this project has now three times found a lock whose test passed without it (`deferred-13`, `deferred-15`, and this one).

- **D12 — `cancelBookingAsParent`'s locked read races a settle-side plain `UPDATE` that carries no lock-timeout hint.** The `jakarta.persistence.lock.timeout` hint applies only to the locked `SELECT`; a plain `UPDATE` issued by a settle in flight ignores it, so under contention the cancel can block rather than surfacing `ApiAdvice`'s clean 409. Architectural, spanning both the cancel and settle paths, and not a regression introduced by this diff — the pre-story code took no lock at all. NOT VALID` followed by `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` — before this table is large in production. [`V94__booking_payment_capture_pending.sql`]

- **D14 — `reserveCapture`'s `REQUIRES_NEW` + `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` opens a second pooled connection per attempted reservation**, held up to the 5 s lock timeout under contention, with no pool-sizing analysis behind it. On the batch path that is one extra connection per credit-funded booking, taken sequentially. Load-dependent and speculative without a concurrency/load test, but it is a new resource pattern on the busiest path and should be measured before the platform carries real volume. Whatever replaces D1 must bring this check with it. [`deploy/backup/prune-backups.sh:150-168`]

## Deferred from: skillars-deferred-23-flaky-perf-test-dead-code-and-ops-hygiene-fixes (2026-08-14)

- **The `jakarta.persistence.lock.timeout` hint has zero effect on PostgreSQL under this project's Hibernate version (6.6.53.Final) — it does not bound any of the four repositories that carry it.** Discovered while implementing this story's AC6, which asked for `SessionPackPurchaseRepository.findByIdForUpdate` to carry the identical hint its three siblings (`CoachProfileRepository`, `BookingRescheduleRequestRepository`, `BookingRepository`) already do, on the stated premise that it makes contention "surface `ApiAdvice`'s clean 409 instead of blocking indefinitely." Traced the mechanism: `org.hibernate.dialect.PostgreSQLDialect.withTimeout(String, int)` only special-cases the `LockOptions.NO_WAIT` and `LockOptions.SKIP_LOCKED` sentinel values; any other timeout (including every finite millisecond value used in this codebase, e.g. `"5000"`) falls through its `default` case and returns the lock string completely unchanged — no `WAIT n` clause (Postgres's `FOR UPDATE` syntax has none) and no `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` statement is ever issued. `PostgreSQLDialect.supportsWait()` returns `false` unconditionally, confirming this is not an oversight in one code path but the dialect's documented position. Confirmed empirically, not just by reading source: a real IT (`SessionPackPurchaseLockTimeoutIT`, written then discarded once this was understood — see this story's `SessionPackPurchaseLockContentionIT` for what replaced it) held a competing row lock for 12 seconds against a `findByIdForUpdate`-backed call carrying the 5000ms hint; the contended call blocked for the entire 12 seconds and then completed normally, with no `PessimisticLockingFailureException` ever thrown. No `lock_timeout`/`statement_timeout`/`idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` is configured anywhere (`application*.yaml`, `docker-compose.yml`) that would bound this some other way. **Consequence:** `ApiAdvice.pessimisticLockExceptionHandler`'s 409 mapping (`ApiAdvice.java:579-582`) is effectively dead code for the finite-timeout case on all four repositories — contention on a `CoachProfile`, `BookingRescheduleRequest`, `Booking`, or `SessionPackPurchase` row genuinely blocks the requesting connection until the actual lock holder releases it, not for a bounded 5 seconds as every one of the four repositories' comments claims. This is a fourth instance of the pattern this project's own history already names three times over (`skillars-deferred-13`, `-15`, `skillars-uat-3`'s own review D11): a lock whose protective claim was never actually exercised by a test with a hold longer than the claimed bound — every existing concurrency IT touching these four repositories (`BookingServiceConcurrencyIT`, `RescheduleResourceIT`'s reschedule-decline race, `PackExtensionIT`'s `extendPack_concurrentRequests_noDuplicateExtension`) holds its competing lock for ≤1.5s, well under the 5s the hint was assumed to bound, so none of them could ever have caught this. **Deliberately not fixed here** — user-directed during this story's implementation (2026-08-14): add the annotation for consistency with the three siblings (matches the AC's literal text), do not expand scope to fix or work around the underlying Hibernate/Postgres gap across four repositories in a story whose AC6 was scoped to one file. A real fix needs a decision, not a patch: candidates include switching all four to `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` + `NO_WAIT` (Postgres does honor this) with an application-level retry/backoff loop, or issuing an explicit `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` natively before the locked query (e.g. via a `@PrePersist`-adjacent hook or a raw statement inside the same transaction) — either touches all four repositories' call sites and needs its own regression proof that the resulting behavior is actually bounded, unlike this hint. [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/marketplace/repo/CoachProfileRepository.java:28-34`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/booking/repo/BookingRescheduleRequestRepository.java:23-31`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/booking/repo/BookingRepository.java:188-193`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/payment/repo/SessionPackPurchaseRepository.java:18-21`]

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-deferred-23-flaky-perf-test-dead-code-and-ops-hygiene-fixes (2026-08-14)

- **AC5's `concurrency` group doesn't guarantee the newest-pushed commit wins `:latest`.** `build-and-push` still declares `needs: test`, so concurrency-slot readiness tracks `test`-job duration, not push order — a slower older push's job can still complete its `build-and-push` after a faster newer push's, overwriting `:latest` with the older commit. The `concurrency` block does prevent two `build-and-push` runs from interleaving mid-upload, a real improvement; it just doesn't close the ordering race the item was originally filed against. Already surfaced and accepted as a deliberate scope decision in this story's own AC5 and the `deferred-work.md` entry it closed (line 1285) — recorded here only per this review's standard defer-logging convention, not a new finding. A stronger fix (`build-and-push` checking `git merge-base --is-ancestor` against the currently-published `:latest` digest before overwriting) remains out of scope. [`.github/workflows/ci.yml:49-53`]
- **`PlaybackServiceIT.authorizePlayback_performance_p99Under200ms` still asserts a hard wall-clock latency bound inside a correctness-gating integration test.** AC1 fixed the two proximate bugs (wrong percentile index, no JIT/connection-pool warmup) that made the assertion flaky, but the underlying design choice — gating build correctness on a millisecond latency threshold in CI, where JIT, GC, and Testcontainers jitter are inherently variable — remains structurally fragile. Pre-existing pattern, not introduced by this story, and fixing it (e.g. moving to a dedicated non-gating perf-tracking job, or asserting a much looser bound) is its own design decision outside AC1's scope. [`src/test/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/video/service/PlaybackServiceIT.java:105-125`]

## Deferred from: skillars-deferred-26-defensive-guards-input-hardening-and-test-coverage-fixes story creation (2026-08-15)

- **D1 — `DrillMetadata.repDensity` cannot represent "coach never set this" at all — it is a Java primitive `int`, not `Integer`.** Found during this story's AC4 spec audit (adversarial review of the story's own AC text, pre-implementation): a missing key in the incoming JSON payload deserializes silently to `0` via Jackson, and an explicit JSON `null` would throw a deserialization exception rather than pass through — so a `repDensity != null` guard anywhere downstream (frontend or backend) can never observe the "unset" case as `null`; it is indistinguishable from a legitimately-zero drill today and will remain so under the current contract. AC4 of this story added a frontend-only defensive guard (protects against `undefined`/`null` arriving from a stale cache, a manually-edited dev fixture, or a future API contract change) but explicitly could not close this gap — doing so needs a backend change: make `repDensity` a nullable `Integer` (propagating through `DrillMetadata`, its JSONB Hibernate mapping, and every backend site that reads it arithmetically) or add an explicit "no density data" signal, plus a decision on whether that is a real product need (do coaches uploading custom drills currently have any path that leaves `repDensity` unset, or does upload validation already require it?). [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/session/contract/DrillMetadata.java:12`, `src/frontend/src/components/session/DrillDetailPanel.vue`]

- **`booking.errors.batchSizeExceeded`'s `{max}` quotes the client's cached batch limit, which is by definition not the limit that rejected the request.** `BookingRequestPage.vue:512` passes `{ max: maxBatchSize.value }`, populated from `getBatchConfig()` at `:553-554` and defaulting to `ref(5)`. The backend's own `messages*.properties` copy deliberately names no figure for this reason. Correct fix is for the backend to pass the real limit as a message argument (`BatchRuleViolationException` currently passes none), which is a contract change, not a mechanical edit. Implemented instead: `BookingRequestPage.vue`'s `booking.batchSizeExceeded` branch now `await getBatchConfig()`s first, assigns the fresh value to `maxBatchSize.value` (which also re-syncs the basket cap for the rest of the session), then toasts with the refreshed number; a new no-number fallback key `booking.errors.batchSizeExceededUnknownMax` covers a failed re-fetch, in all three locale bundles.

## Deferred from: code review of skillars-deferred-29-clockprovider-error-mapping-and-repository-boundary-test-coverage-fixes (2026-08-17)

All three review layers ran and returned findings (Blind Hunter 14, Acceptance Auditor 8, Edge Case Hunter 8) — unlike `skillars-deferred-28`'s run, no layer failed, so this is a complete deferred set for this diff.

- **`BookingRequestPage.submit()`'s `else` branch is the live landing zone for four unmapped, user-actionable pack-selection rejections.** `submit()` always sends `sessionPackPurchaseId: selectedPackId.value`, and `BookingService.createBookingRequest` rejects that id with four distinct errorKeys the if/else chain does not cover: `payment.packExpired` (`BookingService.java:262`), `payment.packCoachMismatch` (`:270`), `payment.packExhausted` (`:273`), and `MISSING_RIGHTS` (`:265` "Pack does not belong to this parent", `:220` "Requested slot is not within coach availability", plus "Coach profile is not active"). Wire values confirmed at `BookingApiAdvice.java:22` (`new ErrorDto(ex.getErrorCode(), new ErrorMsg(ex.getErrorCode(), ex.getErrorCode()))`) and `ApiAdvice.java:271-276`. Concrete symptom: a parent whose pack credits run out between page load and submit sees only "Could not submit request", the pack stays selected, and retrying reproduces it indefinitely. `skillars-deferred-29` AC4 deliberately scoped itself to adding a `console.warn` here rather than closing the mapping gap, so the gap is now logged instead of fixed. Closing this needs four new branches plus i18n keys — the same shape as, and naturally bundled with, the coach-side accept-flow item at `:1461`. [`src/frontend/src/pages/parent/BookingRequestPage.vue:489-492`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/booking/service/BookingService.java:220,262,265,270,273`] `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-30 AC2]` Added five branches (a fifth, `payment.coachStripeNotConfigured`, was found during this story's own review and folded in): `payment.coachStripeNotConfigured`, `payment.packExpired`, `payment.packCoachMismatch`, `payment.packExhausted`, `MISSING_RIGHTS`. Reused existing i18n keys (`payment.error.coachStripeNotConfigured`, `payment.sessionPack.packCoachMismatch`, `payment.sessionPack.packExhausted`) wherever they already existed; added exactly two new keys under `booking.errors` (`packExpired`, `requestNotAllowed`) in all three locale bundles. `submitBatchRequest()` confirmed unchanged — `BookingBatchService.createBatch` never resolves a session pack or calls the payment gateway.

- **[OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC3] `ParentBookingsPage.submitReschedule()`'s `else` branch swallows `MISSING_RIGHTS`, which covers four distinct reschedule rejections including the most common one.** `RescheduleService.requestReschedule` throws `SecurityError.MISSING_RIGHTS` at `:61`, `:65`, `:69` and `:96` — booking not in CONFIRMED/UPCOMING, proposed start not in the future, proposed end not after start, and `:96`'s `OperationNotAllowedException("A pending reschedule request already exists", SecurityError.MISSING_RIGHTS)`. Only `:88` uses `BookingError.INVALID_SESSION_DURATION`, the single code the page maps. Symptom: a parent submitting a second reschedule while one is still PENDING gets "Reschedule request failed", the dialog stays open (`rescheduleDialogOpen` is cleared only on success at `:207`), and they can loop forever. All four conditions also now emit an identical `[booking] unmapped errorKey: MISSING_RIGHTS` line, so the new log cannot distinguish them either — closing this properly needs distinct backend error codes, not just frontend branches, which makes it a contract change rather than a mechanical fix. [`src/frontend/src/pages/parent/ParentBookingsPage.vue:213-216`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/booking/service/RescheduleService.java:61,65,69,88,96`] `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-31 AC3 — scope widened]` The item named only the parent request path; re-enumerating every `SecurityError.MISSING_RIGHTS` throw in `RescheduleService` found **twelve** across all three public methods, of which only three (one ownership check each in `requestReschedule`, `acceptReschedule`, `declineReschedule`) are genuine authorization. The other nine were re-coded to three new `BookingError` constants (`BOOKING_NOT_RESCHEDULABLE`, `RESCHEDULE_ALREADY_PENDING`, `RESCHEDULE_NOT_PENDING`) plus the existing `START_TIME_IN_PAST` / `INVALID_TIME_RANGE`, all three new wire codes added to all four `messages*.properties` and all three frontend bundles. Frontend branches added to `ParentBookingsPage.submitReschedule` (5 new in total, one of which is a `MISSING_RIGHTS` branch that previously fell through to the generic `else`), `CoachCommandCenterPage.handleAcceptReschedule` (4 new, likewise including `MISSING_RIGHTS`) and `handleDeclineReschedule` (1 new, on a method that had a bare `catch {}` with no `errorKey` read at all). Post-change grep confirms exactly three `MISSING_RIGHTS` throws remain. `RescheduleResourceIT` had zero assertions on any reschedule `errorKey` before this story, so two mutation-verified ITs were added — one parent-path (`booking.notReschedulable`) and one decline-path (`booking.rescheduleNotPending`, the site a mid-file truncated enumeration misses).

- **`DrillDetailPanel`'s `else` branch tells the coach to "try again" for two deterministically non-retryable rejections.** `checkDrillUploadGate` throws `FeatureGatedException` when the coach's subscription tier lacks drill upload (`DrillUploadService.java:135-141`, mapped at `ApiAdvice.java:330` to errorKey `security.featureGated`), and a READY video already linked to the drill throws `OperationNotAllowedException(SessionErrorCode.DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED)` (`DrillUploadService.java:75-79`, mapped at `ApiAdvice.java:273-274` to errorKey `DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED`). Both display `en-US/index.js:343`'s "Upload failed. Please try again.", and the `finally` block clears `selectedVideoFile.value = null`, so the coach must re-pick the file for a retry guaranteed to fail identically. The feature-gated case is the worse of the two — it should route the coach to an upgrade, not to a retry. Same bug class as `skillars-deferred-29` AC2 fixed for the quota/constraint branches, on the same catch block, deliberately left out of that AC's scope. Feature-gated branch reuses the existing `security.featureGated` key (no upgrade CTA added, per this AC's own scope decision). `DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED` branch adds a new key `session.drillLibrary.upload.videoAlreadyLinked` to all three locale bundles. Note this code is not unique to "already linked" — `initiateUpload`'s drill-ownership check throws the identical code, currently unreachable from this panel by accident of today's data flow, not by contract; see the new ledger item below for the real fix (splitting the code).

- **`'QUOTA_EXCEEDED'` cannot distinguish a transient upload rate limit from a hard storage quota, so the toast promises an upgrade for a condition that clears itself in a minute.** `VideoService.java:231` (`throw new QuotaExceededException(request.ownerId(), "rate limit exceeded")`) and `:249` (`throw new QuotaExceededException(request.ownerId(), 0L, request.fileSizeBytes())`) throw the same exception type, both land in `VideoApiAdvice.java:75`'s `videoQuotaExceededHandler`, and both emit `errorMsg.errorKey = "QUOTA_EXCEEDED"`. `skillars-deferred-29` AC2 made this newly reachable: pre-fix the branch was dead (`helpCode` carried a per-request Sqids support id), so both paths fell to the generic "Upload failed" toast — vague, but not actively misleading. A coach who clicks upload more than `app.video.upload.rate-limit.requests-per-minute` times in a minute is now told "Storage quota exceeded. Upgrade your plan to upload more videos." Correct fix is a backend contract change splitting `VideoErrorCode.QUOTA_EXCEEDED` into a rate-limit code and a storage-quota code, which is why it was not closed inside a bundled small-fix story. [`src/frontend/src/components/session/DrillDetailPanel.vue:384`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/video/service/VideoService.java:231,249`, `src/frontend/src/i18n/en-US/index.js:341`] `[MITIGATED by skillars-deferred-29 code review response — 2026-08-17]` Reworded all three locale bundles' `quotaExceeded` string to "Upload limit reached. Try again in a moment, or upgrade your plan for more storage." (and FR/DE equivalents), so the toast no longer promises an upgrade for what may be a transient rate limit. The underlying conflation (one wire code, two distinct causes) is NOT closed — that still needs the backend contract change described above; this only fixes the misleading copy. `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-32 AC2]` **Two corrections to this item's own claims, found during `skillars-deferred-32`'s re-verification and its code review:** the exact toast text quoted above no longer exists (superseded by the `MITIGATED` rewording noted just above); and the exception is thrown at **four** sites across **two** classes, not the two `VideoService` lines cited — `VideoService.java:135` (`retryUpload`, missed by the item) and `QuotaService.java:76` (the post-`SELECT FOR UPDATE` gate, `quotaProvider.check`'s own comment names it "the authoritative gate") both also carry `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`. Implemented: new `VideoErrorCode.UPLOAD_RATE_LIMITED`, thrown only at the `:231` rate-limit site via a new `RateLimitExceededException`; `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` keeps all three storage sites unchanged (`VideoService.java:135,249`, `QuotaService.java:76`). New `VideoApiAdvice` handler (429, unchanged status — see the new ledger item below), message key `video.rateLimitExceeded` in all four backend bundles. `DrillDetailPanel.vue` gained an `UPLOAD_RATE_LIMITED` branch → new `session.drillLibrary.upload.rateLimited` key; the existing `quotaExceeded` key dropped its rate-limit hedge and became storage-only. Both in all three frontend bundles.

- **`findActivePacks`' `p.coachId = :coachId` predicate is still unproven by any real-database test.** Every one of the five rows `findActivePacks_excludesExhaustedExpiredPausedAndOtherPlayerPacks` seeds uses the same `coach.getId()` — only the *player* is varied. Delete `AND p.coachId = :coachId` from the JPQL and both tests in the class stay green. Undetected symptom would be cross-coach credit leakage: a parent holding packs with coach A and coach B gets coach A's credits offered when booking coach B. `skillars-deferred-29` AC5 explicitly accepted this as narrower-than-ideal scope (a second full coach+profile fixture was judged disproportionate to a bundled-fix story) and its own Dev Notes name it as "the one remaining boundary gap" — recorded here so a future story revisiting `findActivePacks` picks it up rather than rediscovering it. Mutation-verified: temporarily deleting `AND p.coachId = :coachId` from the JPQL failed the new assertion as expected, then reverted.

- **[OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC6] The three `console.warn(..., err)` calls fire on every transport failure and every 401 redirect, and serialize the request body — including a minor's `playerId` and free-text `notes` — into the browser console.** `boot/axios.js:177` rejects with the original `AxiosError` unconditionally, including for failures it already handled: on a network drop `error.response` is undefined so the warn prints `[booking] unmapped errorKey: undefined`, and on 401 session expiry the interceptor has already set `window.location.href` (`:140-153`) yet the warn plus a "submit failed" toast still fire during the redirect. The logged `err` carries `err.config.data`, the serialized booking payload built at `BookingRequestPage.vue:472-479`. No auth-token exposure — this app uses cookie-based auth and the request interceptor sets only `Accept-Language` — so the leak is limited to request-body PII in the user's own console buffer. `skillars-deferred-29` AC4 prescribed the `err` argument deliberately, having reasoned explicitly about the `undefined` noise and judged the second argument the cheapest way to distinguish signal from transport failure; changing it now would contradict a documented decision. Revisit if a console-capturing telemetry integration (Sentry breadcrumbs, LogRocket) is ever added, at which point the PII exposure stops being local. The warnings themselves are kept: they are the `skillars-deferred-29` AC4 fix that makes an unmapped code visible at all. The `undefined`-on-transport-failure and 401-redirect noise this item also describes is unchanged and not claimed closed.
