- **`SluDashboardServiceTest`'s three `getWeeklyExposure_*` tests mirror the production date expression, so they cannot detect a change to it — and the new fixed clock is pinned to a date that exercises nothing the old wall clock didn't.** The tests compute expected `fromYear`/`fromWeek` with `now.minusWeeks(8 - 1)`, byte-identical to `SluDashboardService`'s `now.minusWeeks(weeksBack - 1)`, and `skillars-deferred-29` AC1 edited both sides in lockstep when removing `.with(DayOfWeek.MONDAY)`. An off-by-one introduced later (`minusWeeks(weeksBack)`) moves the expectation with the code and stays green; these tests verify the repository is called and results are mapped, not the date arithmetic. Relatedly, all three pin `Instant.parse("2026-08-19T10:00:00Z")` — a Wednesday in ISO week 34 of 2026, with `from` in week 27 of the same ISO year — so the compound `(year, week)` range predicate is never exercised across an ISO week-based-year rollover, which is the one case a fixed clock exists to reach cheaply and a real clock reaches roughly twice a year. Pinning e.g. `2027-01-06T10:00:00Z` (ISO week 1 of 2027, `from` in week 47 of 2026) would earn the abstraction, but only once the mirroring is broken by asserting against literal expected values. AC1 explicitly directed that the mirror be preserved exactly, so this is out of that story's scope by construction. [`src/test/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/development/service/SluDashboardServiceTest.java:56-60,84-88,117-121`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/development/service/SluDashboardService.java:41-44`] `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-30 AC5]` Re-pinned all three tests' fixed clock to `2027-01-06T10:00:00Z` and replaced the computed `eq(fromYear)`/`eq(fromWeek)`/`eq(curYear)`/`eq(curWeek)` arguments with hardcoded literals (`curYear=2027, curWeek=1, fromYear=2026, fromWeek=47`, independently re-verified via a real JVM computation). Mutation-verified: temporarily changing production's `minusWeeks(weeksBack - 1)` to `minusWeeks(weeksBack)` failed the tests as expected, then reverted. Note this does NOT add coverage for the JPQL `(year, week)` predicate itself — that gap is filed as a new item below, since this AC's mechanism (literal values) and its hygiene choice (rollover-spanning date) are distinct, and only the former is what actually breaks the self-mirroring.

## Deferred from: skillars-deferred-30 story creation and review (2026-08-18)

Found while scoping/reviewing `skillars-deferred-30`. The story's senior-dev review (`story-review.md`)
caught that the draft's AC1, AC2, AC3 and AC5 each rested on a premise a fuller re-read of the throw sites
disproved (AC1's error code was not unique to one condition, AC2 had missed a fifth unmapped code, AC3
prescribed a branch on a code the batch loop swallows, AC5 misjudged what its fixture proved). Three of
those corrections produced one filed item each; the fourth item below was found separately while examining
AC3's three catch blocks and is not a premise correction. All are filed rather than fixed because each
needs a backend contract change or its own dedicated test-writing effort, outside a bundled small-fix
story's bar. **Two of the four have since been closed by that story's own code review — see the
`[CLOSED …]` markers inline.**

- **[OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC2] `BookingBatchService.acceptAll` returns HTTP 200 with a positive "All sessions accepted" toast even when zero bookings in the batch were actually accepted.** Found while scoping `skillars-deferred-30` AC3 (coach-side accept-flow error mapping): `acceptAll`'s per-booking loop (`BookingBatchService.java:263-278`) wraps `acceptOneBooking` in `try { … } catch (Exception e) { log.warn(...) }` with no rethrow — the method's own comment already documents this as intentional ("the per-booking throws are swallowed by the loop's catch and the caller sees a silent no-op"). When every booking fails, `acceptedIds` stays empty, `acceptAll` hits `if (acceptedIds.isEmpty()) { log.warn(...); return; }` (`:280-283`) and returns normally — no exception, no non-2xx status. `CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue:179`'s `handleAcceptAll` then fires `$q.notify({ message: t('booking.batch.acceptedAll'), type: 'positive' })` unconditionally on a 2xx response. A coach attempting to accept a batch where every slot has since become unavailable (or the coach itself is suspended mid-batch) is told "All sessions accepted" while nothing changed. A partial success (e.g. 3 of 5) reports identically. Real fix needs `acceptAll` to return per-booking outcomes (which slots succeeded/failed and why) or at minimum a non-2xx status when `acceptedIds` is empty — a backend contract change, not a frontend patch, and outside `skillars-deferred-30`'s bundled-fix bar (see that story's AC3 for the narrower fix it does ship: a `booking.coachUnavailable` branch for the one code that *can* reach the client from this flow, via the pre-flight suspension check before the loop). [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/booking/service/BookingBatchService.java:242-283`, `src/frontend/src/pages/coach/CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue:174-186`] `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-31 AC2 — false-success half only]` Replaced the silent `return` at `BookingBatchService.java:281-284` with `throw new OperationNotAllowedException("No bookings in batch were accepted", BookingError.BATCH_NONE_ACCEPTED)` (new enum constant → wire code `booking.batchNoneAccepted`, in all four `messages*.properties`), mapped to a new `booking.errors.batchNoneAccepted` branch in `handleAcceptAll`. `OperationNotAllowedException` maps to 403 regardless of code, so no status-code change beyond 2xx→403 on this one branch. The existing `log.warn` is kept — it is the only per-booking diagnostic that survives the loop's swallow — and a code comment now names both paths that reach the branch (every per-booking accept threw, or a still-PENDING batch had no REQUESTED bookings) so it is not read as only "all failed". `handleAcceptAll`'s throw-site comment was updated from three reachable outcomes to four. Two mutation-verified unit tests cover both reaching paths. **Per-booking outcome reporting is NOT closed and is re-filed as its own item below.**

- **`SessionErrorCode.DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED` is thrown by three unrelated conditions inside `DrillUploadService`, and the frontend cannot distinguish them from the wire `errorKey` alone.** Found while scoping `skillars-deferred-30` AC1 (non-retryable video-upload rejection toasts): `initiateUpload`'s drill-ownership check (`DrillUploadService.java:56-57`, `"Drill upload not allowed"` — coach doesn't own this `COACH`-library drill) and its already-linked-video check (`:75-78`, `"A video is already linked to this drill. Remove it before uploading a new one."` — a `READY` video already exists) both throw `OperationNotAllowedException(..., SessionErrorCode.DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED)`, and `SessionErrorCode.getErrorCode()` returns the bare enum name for both, so the wire `errorKey` is identical either way. `skillars-deferred-30` AC1 ships a message specific to the already-linked case anyway, because the ownership-check path is **not reachable from the panel today** — `DrillLibraryService.list` scopes non-`PLATFORM` results to the calling coach's own drills (`findByOwnerCoachIdAndStatus`, `DrillLibraryService.java:70`) and `DrillDetailPanel.vue`'s template independently gates on `libraryType === 'COACH'` (`:94,241`) — but that correctness is an accident of today's data flow, not a guarantee the wire contract makes. Any future caller that reaches `initiateUpload` with an id the panel didn't source from the scoped listing (a shared library, a team-drill feature, a different frontend surface) would show the already-linked message for an ownership rejection. Real fix is splitting `DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED` into distinct `SessionErrorCode` values, one per throw site — a backend contract change, outside `skillars-deferred-30`'s bundled-fix bar. [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/session/service/DrillUploadService.java:56-57,75-79`, `src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/session/contract/SessionErrorCode.java`] **CORRECTED by the `skillars-deferred-30` code review (2026-08-18):** there are three throw sites, not two — `DrillUploadService.java:57` (drill-ownership check in `initiateUpload`), `:78` (a READY video already linked), and `:109` (the same ownership check again, in `deleteVideo`). `story-review.md` B3's own table listed all three; the item dropped one when it was filed. This matters for the prescribed fix: "one code per throw site → two codes" would leave `deleteVideo` sharing whichever code it inherits and re-open the ambiguity. Three sites collapse to two distinct *causes* (ownership, already-linked), so the split should be by cause — one ownership code used at `:57` and `:109`, one already-linked code at `:78`. `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-32 AC1]` Implemented exactly the corrected split: new `SessionErrorCode.DRILL_NOT_OWNED` at `:57` and `:109`, new `SessionErrorCode.DRILL_VIDEO_ALREADY_LINKED` at `:78`; `DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED` deleted from the enum (`grep -rn "DRILL_UPLOAD_NOT_ALLOWED" src/` returns zero hits). `DrillDetailPanel.vue`'s upload chain gained two branches — the existing `session.drillLibrary.upload.videoAlreadyLinked` key unchanged, plus a new `session.drillLibrary.upload.notOwned` key in all three bundles. `errorKey` assertions added to the three existing `DrillUploadResourceIT` cases that already proved 403 (`initiateUpload_platformDrill_returns403`, `initiateUpload_otherCoachDrill_returns403`, `deleteVideo_platformDrill_returns403`), plus one new IT proving a `READY` linked video returns `DRILL_VIDEO_ALREADY_LINKED`. Mutation-verified by swapping the two constants at the ownership and already-linked throw sites and confirming all four ITs fail, then restoring byte-identical.

- **[OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC5] `SluWeeklySnapshotRepository.findByPlayerIdFromWeek`'s compound `(year, week)` range predicate has zero repository-level test coverage at any date.** Found while scoping `skillars-deferred-30` AC5 (breaking `SluDashboardServiceTest`'s self-mirroring formula assertions): `grep -rn "findByPlayerIdFromWeek" src/test/` returns only three mocked stubs inside `SluDashboardServiceTest`, a pure Mockito unit test (`@Mock private SluWeeklySnapshotRepository snapshotRepository`) that never executes the real JPQL. The predicate itself — `(s.id.isoYear > :fromYear OR (s.id.isoYear = :fromYear AND s.id.isoWeek >= :fromWeek)) AND (s.id.isoYear < :toYear OR (s.id.isoYear = :toYear AND s.id.isoWeek <= :toWeek))` (`SluWeeklySnapshotRepository.java:30-33`) — looks correct by inspection for an ISO week-based-year rollover (e.g. `(2026,47) → (2027,01)`), but "looks correct by inspection" is exactly the gap a real IT closes and nothing here does. `skillars-deferred-30` AC5 re-pins `SluDashboardServiceTest`'s mock-level clock to a rollover-spanning date for an unrelated reason (breaking the test's formula-mirroring, not exercising this query), and explicitly does not claim to close this gap. Real fix is a new `SluWeeklySnapshotRepositoryIT` seeding `player_skill_stats`/`player_slu_weekly_snapshot` rows either side of an ISO week-based-year boundary and asserting the correct rows come back — a dedicated test-writing task, outside `skillars-deferred-30`'s bundled-fix bar. [`src/main/java/com/softropic/skillars/platform/development/repo/SluWeeklySnapshotRepository.java:30-33`] `[CLOSED by skillars-deferred-31 AC5]` New `SluWeeklySnapshotRepositoryIT` (5 tests, fixture block `9630000001`–`9630000003`) extending `AbstractIntegrationTest`: same-year window with both boundaries inclusive and rows excluded either side; a `(2025,52) → (2026,2)` rollover window that a naive `week BETWEEN from AND to` returns nothing for; a two-rollover window proving a whole intervening year comes back regardless of its week numbers; a foreign-`playerId` row inside the window excluded; and ascending `(isoYear, isoWeek)` ordering asserted against deliberately reversed insert order. Mutation-verified four ways — dropping either `isoYear` disjunct fails 3 of 5, making both week boundaries exclusive fails 4 of 5, neutralising the `playerId` predicate fails 1 — then the JPQL was restored byte-identical. No production code changed.

- **[WITHDRAWN by the `skillars-deferred-30` code review, 2026-08-18 — the described defect cannot occur.] All three coach-side booking-accept flows report a post-success refresh failure as an accept failure.** This item was filed on the premise that each flow `await`s a data-refresh call inside the same `try` as the mutating accept call, so a failing refresh `GET` would trip the shared `catch` and tell the coach an accepted booking had failed. The premise is wrong: all three refresh loaders swallow their own errors and never rethrow — `loadParentBookings` (`booking.store.js:289-300`), `loadCoachBookingRequests` (`:302-314`) and `loadCoachSchedule` (`:316-328`) each `catch (e) { xError.value = e }` with no `throw`. A refresh failure therefore cannot reach `approveBooking`'s, `handleAcceptAllBatch`'s or `handleAcceptReschedule`'s catch at all, and no toast is reversed. **The real residual is the opposite one and remains open:** a failed refresh is silent — the coach sees the success toast, the list quietly stays stale, and the `coachRequestsError` / `coachScheduleError` refs that were just set are never rendered by any caller. Worth a small fix that surfaces "list may be out of date, reload" when a post-mutation refresh fails. [`src/frontend/src/stores/booking.store.js:289-328`, `src/frontend/src/pages/coach/CoachCommandCenterPage.vue:373-376`] **[Its live residual — the silent post-mutation refresh failure, and the fact that `coachRequestsError`/`coachScheduleError` are rendered by no component — is OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC1.]**

Note this branch's fallback key (`booking.batch.submitError`) *does* resolve, unlike `submit()`'s. Resolution is coupled to the `MISSING_RIGHTS` decision recorded on the story: if the answer there is "split the backend codes rather than branch on an overloaded one", this item is closed by that same backend change rather than by a frontend branch. That check lives in `acceptAll` (`BookingBatchService.java:245-247`), a different method — `createBatch` has no such check. `createBatch`'s four causes are the ones named above; `BATCH_ALREADY_PROCESSED` is a fifth, re-coded in `acceptAll` and surfaced by `handleAcceptAll`, not `submitBatchRequest()`. `MISSING_RIGHTS` still reaches that chain, but now only from the two genuine authorization sites, where one authorization-worded toast is the correct mapping rather than a swallowed distinction.
- **[OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC1] The three coach-side accept flows now have three different post-failure refresh behaviours, and one of them toasts before the state it describes.** `skillars-deferred-30` AC3 touched all three catch blocks without normalising what happens after the toast: `CoachBookingRequestsPage.handleAccept` notifies and *then* `await`s `loadCoachBookingRequests()`, so on a `booking.slotUnavailable` rejection the coach reads "This time slot is no longer available" while the row is still rendered as pending, and a second click on the still-visible row fires another doomed request; `handleAcceptAll` and `CoachCommandCenterPage.handleAcceptReschedule` do not refresh at all after a failure, so their lists stay stale indefinitely. Related but distinct from the pre-existing item at the `skillars-deferred-30` story-review entry above: that one concerns refresh calls sharing the mutating call's `try`, this one concerns the inconsistency and the toast/reload ordering. A fix should pick one behaviour for all three. Each individual choice is defensible (AC2 deliberately reused two existing, previously-unused `payment.sessionPack.*` keys rather than shipping near-duplicates, which was the right call), but the aggregate leaves a payment-domain string sitting in the booking namespace between two siblings that live under payment, and the next person adding a `payment.packSomething` branch has three precedents and no principle. No behaviour change.
- **[OWNED BY skillars-deferred-31 AC7] `CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue`'s `handleAccept` calls its post-failure refresh unguarded — a rejecting refresh throws an unhandled promise rejection out of the catch block.** Found by a second, independent code review pass (2026-08-18): `await bookingStore.loadCoachBookingRequests()` at `CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue:164` sits inside `handleAccept`'s `catch` block with no try/catch of its own. If the accept call fails AND the immediately-following refresh call also fails (e.g. a transient network blip right after a real rejection), the refresh's rejection propagates unhandled rather than being absorbed — the coach's toast for the original failure still fires (it's already been queued before the `await`), but the unhandled rejection is a latent robustness gap a browser console/error-monitoring integration would surface as noise at best, an uncaught exception at worst. Pre-existing pattern, not introduced by `skillars-deferred-30`'s edits (which only added `errorKey` branching before this line). A one-line `.catch(() => {})` closes it. [`src/frontend/src/pages/coach/CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue:164`] `[WITHDRAWN by skillars-deferred-31 AC7 — the described defect cannot occur]` The item rests on the premise that `loadCoachBookingRequests()` can reject. It cannot: `booking.store.js:302-314` wraps the whole call in `try { … } catch (e) { coachRequestsError.value = e }` with **no rethrow**, so the `await` at `CoachBookingRequestsPage.vue:164` always resolves and no unhandled rejection is possible. The prescribed `.catch(() => {})` would be dead code, and a future reader would have to re-derive why it is there. The identical unguarded pattern in `handleDecline` (`:176`) is harmless for exactly the same reason and was likewise not "fixed". This is the **same false premise** the `skillars-deferred-30` code review withdrew a sibling item for on the same day, reached independently by a different review pass hours later — the store's swallow-and-never-rethrow contract is evidently not obvious from the call sites, so AC7 also added a CONTRACT comment above `loadCoachBookingRequests`/`loadCoachSchedule` in `booking.store.js` stating that neither loader ever rethrows and callers therefore need no guard. The live residual these two withdrawn items were both circling — a silent failed refresh — is closed by AC1.

Closing this needs `acceptAll` to return a per-booking result DTO — which booking ids settled, which did not, and the `ErrorCode` for each — which is a REST contract change (currently `void` → 204), a store change, and new rendering on `CoachBookingRequestsPage` for a partial outcome. The total-failure path (`booking.batchNoneAccepted`) is unchanged. - **`booking.errors.requestNotAllowed` is worded for the parent booking-request path but is now the `MISSING_RIGHTS` message on four coach- and reschedule-side paths too.** The string is *"You do not have access to the player or session pack in this request."* — accurate for `BookingRequestPage.submit()`, where `MISSING_RIGHTS` genuinely means the caller does not own the player profile or the session pack. `skillars-deferred-30` AC3 and `skillars-deferred-31` AC3 reused the same key for `CoachBookingRequestsPage.handleAcceptAll` (coach does not own the *batch*), `CoachCommandCenterPage.handleAcceptReschedule` (coach does not own the *booking*) and `ParentBookingsPage.submitReschedule` (parent does not own the *booking*), where "player or session pack" names the wrong object. Both stories reused the key deliberately rather than shipping a near-duplicate with no rule for choosing between them.
